### 17.1.5 Linearization of nonlinear relationship

< Fig. 17.8 a) > ill-suited for linear least-square regression

· Exponential model

$$y = a_1 e^{b_1 x} \rightarrow \text{ln } y = \text{ln } a_1 + b_1 x$$

· Power equation

$$y = a_2 x^{b_2} \rightarrow \log y = \log a_2 + b_2 \log x$$

• Saturation-growth-rate-equation ∫ ⊆ ?

$$y = a_3 \frac{x}{b_3 + x} \rightarrow \frac{1}{y} = \frac{b_3}{a_3} \frac{1}{x} + \frac{1}{a_3};$$
 

· Linearization

$$\ln y = \ln a_1 + b_1 \ln e$$

$$\ln y = \ln a_1 + b_1 x$$

$$\log y = b_2 \ln x + \log a_2$$

$$\frac{1}{v} = \frac{b_3}{a_2} \frac{1}{x} + \frac{1}{a_3}$$

[Ex. 17.4]

### 17.1.6 General Comments on Linear Regression

- · Some statistical assumption that are inherent in the linear L.S. procedure are
- 1) x has a fixed value : it is not random and is measured without error
- 2) The y values are independent random variables and all have the same variance.
- 3) The y values for a given x must be normally distributed.

### 17.2 Polynomial Regression ( 약황성 화귀 분명)

· m<sup>th</sup> degree polynomial

$$y = a_0 + a_1 x + a_2 x^2 + \dots + a_m x^m + e$$

$$e = y - (a_0 + a_1 x + a_2 x^2 + \dots + a_m x^m)$$

· sum of squares of the residuals

$$Z e^{-x}$$
 >  $S_r = \sum_{i=1}^n (y_i - a_0 - a_1 x_i - a_2 x_i^2 - \dots - a_m x_i^m)^2$   $S_r$  이 최소가 되는  $a_i = ?$ 

· Differentiation : Minimize the error

$$\begin{cases} \frac{\partial S_r}{\partial a_0} = -2\sum (y_i - a_0 - a_1 x_i - a_2 x_i^2 - \dots - a_m x_i^m)^2 = 0 \\ \frac{\partial S_r}{\partial a_1} = -2\sum x_i (y_i - a_0 - a_1 x_i - a_2 x_i^2 - \dots - a_m x_i^m)^2 = 0 \\ \frac{\partial S_r}{\partial a_2} = -2\sum x_i^2 (y_i - a_0 - a_1 x_i - a_2 x_i^2 - \dots - a_m x_i^m)^2 = 0 \\ \vdots \\ \frac{\partial S_r}{\partial a_m} = -2\sum x_i^m (y_i - a_0 - a_1 x_i - a_2 x_i^2 - \dots - a_m x_i^m)^2 = 0 \end{cases}$$

Rearrange

$$\begin{vmatrix} a_0n + a_1 \sum x_i + a_2 \sum x_i^2 + \cdots + a_m \sum x_i^m = \sum y_i \\ a_0 \sum x_i + a_1 \sum x_i^2 + a_2 \sum x_i^3 + \cdots + a_m \sum x_i^{m+1} = \sum x_i y_i \\ a_0 \sum x_i^2 + a_1 \sum x_i^3 + a_2 \sum x_i^4 + \cdots + a_m \sum x_i^{m+2} = \sum x_i^2 y_i \\ \vdots \\ a_0 \sum x_i^m + a_1 \sum x_i^{m+1} + a_2 \sum x_i^{m+2} + \cdots + a_m \sum x_i^{2m} = \sum x_i^m y_i$$
 
$$\begin{vmatrix} n & \sum x_i & \sum x_i^2 & \cdots & \sum x_i^m \\ \sum x_i & \sum x_i^2 & \sum x_i^3 & \cdots & \sum x_i^{m+1} \\ \sum x_i^2 & \sum x_i^3 & \sum x_i^4 & \cdots & \sum x_i^{m+2} \\ \vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \vdots \\ \sum x_i^m & \sum x_i^{m+1} & \sum x_i^{m+2} & \cdots & \sum x_i^{2m} \\ \vdots & \vdots & \vdots \\ \sum x_i^m & \sum x_i^{m+1} & \sum x_i^{m+2} & \cdots & \sum x_i^{2m} \\ \end{vmatrix} \begin{vmatrix} a_0 & \sum y_i \\ a_1 & a_2 \\ a_2 & = \sum x_i^2 y_i \\ \vdots & \vdots \\ \sum x_i^m y_i \\ \vdots & \vdots \\ \sum x_i^m & \sum x_i^{m+1} & \sum x_i^{m+2} & \cdots & \sum x_i^{2m} \\ \vdots & \vdots & \vdots \\ \sum x_i^m & \sum x_i^{m+1} & \sum x_i^{m+2} & \cdots & \sum x_i^{2m} \\ \end{vmatrix} \begin{vmatrix} a_0 & \sum x_i y_i \\ a_2 \\ \vdots & \vdots \\ a_m & \sum x_i^2 y_i \\ \vdots & \vdots \\ \sum x_i^m & \sum x_i^m & \sum x_i^{m+1} & \sum x_i^{m+2} & \cdots & \sum x_i^{2m} \\ a_m & \sum x_i^m & \sum x_i^m & a_m \end{vmatrix}$$

### 17.3 Multiple Linear Regression

[ Fig. 17.16 ]

2-D or 3-D Prob.

Ex) 
$$z = f(x, y)$$
 or  $w = f(x, y, z)$ 

• Linear function 
$$y = a_0 + a_1x_1 + a_2x_2 + e$$

· Sum of the square of the residuals

$$S_r = \sum_{i=1}^n (y_i - a_0 - a_1 x_{1i} - a_2 x_{2i})^2$$

· Differentiation

$$\begin{cases} \frac{\partial S_r}{\partial a_0} = -2\sum (y_i - a_0 - a_1 x_{1i} - a_2 x_{2i}) = 0\\ \frac{\partial S_r}{\partial a_1} = -2\sum x_{1i}(y_i - a_0 - a_1 x_{1i} - a_2 x_{2i}) = 0\\ \frac{\partial S_r}{\partial a_2} = -2\sum x_{2i}(y_i - a_0 - a_1 x_{1i} - a_2 x_{2i}) = 0 \end{cases}$$

· Rearrange

$$\begin{cases} na_0 + \sum x_{1i} a_1 + \sum x_{2i} a_2 = \sum y_i \\ \sum x_{1i} a_0 + \sum x_{1i}^2 a_1 + \sum x_{1i} x_{2i} a_2 = \sum x_{1i} y_i \\ \sum x_{2i} a_0 + \sum x_{1i} x_{2i} a_1 + \sum x_{2i}^2 a_2 = \sum x_{2i} y_i \\ \sum x_{1i} \sum x_{1i}^2 \sum x_{1i} x_{2i} \\ \sum x_{2i} \sum x_{1i} x_{2i} \\ \sum x_{2i}^2 \sum x_{2i} y_i \end{cases}$$

· General case ( W-P).

$$y = a_0 + a_1 x_1 + a_2 x_2 + \dots + a_m x_m + e$$

$$\begin{bmatrix} n & \sum x_{1i} & \sum x_{2i} - \cdots & \sum x_{mi} \\ \sum x_{1i} & \sum x_{1i}^{2} & \sum x_{1i}x_{2i} - \cdots & \sum x_{1i}x_{mi} \\ \sum x_{2i} & \sum x_{1i}x_{2i} & \sum x_{2i}^{2} - \cdots & \sum x_{2i}x_{mi} \\ \vdots & \sum x_{mi} & \sum x_{mi}x_{1i} & \sum x_{mi}x_{2i} - \cdots & \sum x_{mi}^{2} \end{bmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} a_{0} \\ a_{1} \\ a_{2} \\ \vdots \\ a_{m} \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} \sum y_{i} \\ \sum x_{1i}y_{i} \\ \sum x_{2i}y_{i} \\ \vdots \\ \sum x_{mi}y_{i} \end{pmatrix}$$

: Symmetric matrix

o Standard error of the estimate

$$S_{x/y} = \sqrt{\frac{S_r}{n - (m+1)}}$$
,  $r = \sqrt{\frac{S_t - S_r}{S_t}}$ ; Coefficient of determination.

[Fig. 17.17 ] - Pseudo-codes for multiple regression

· Power equation of general form

$$y = a_0 x_1^{a_1} x_2^{a_2} \cdots x_m^{a_m}$$

$$\log y = \log a_0 + a_1 \log x_1 + a_2 \log x_2 + \dots + a_m \log x_m$$

### 17.4 General Linear Least Squares

### 17.4.1 General matrix formulation for linear least squares

Function

$$y = a_0 z_0 + a_1 z_1 + a_2 z_2 + \dots + a_m z_m + e$$

multiple

$$z_0 = 1$$
,  $z_1 = x_1$ ,  $z_2 = x_2$ , ...,  $z_m = x_m$ 

simple monomial

$$z_0 = x^0 = 1$$
,  $z_1 = x$ ,  $z_2 = x^2$ , ...,  $z_m = x^m$ 

; 
$$\Rightarrow$$
  $z_i = f(x_i)$  : Known functional relation

Matrix

$$\{Y\} = [Z]\{A\} + \{E\}$$

$$[Z] = \begin{bmatrix} z_{01} & z_{11} & \cdots & z_{m1} \\ z_{02} & z_{12} & \cdots & z_{m2} \\ \vdots & & & & \\ z_{0n} & z_{1n} & \cdots & z_{mn} \end{bmatrix}, \quad \{Y\}^T = [y_1 \ y_2 \ \cdots \ y_n], \quad \{A\}^T = [a_0 \ a_1 \ \cdots \ a_m]$$

$$\{E\}^T = [\ e_1 \ e_2 \ \cdots \ e_n] \quad m \quad ; \quad {\rm order} \quad {\rm of} \quad {\rm regression}, \quad n \quad ; \quad {\rm number} \quad {\rm of} \quad {\rm data},$$

$$z_{mi} = f_m(x_i)$$

$$S_r = \sum_{i=1}^n (y_i - \sum_{j=0}^m a_j z_{j,i})^2$$

· Differentiation and rearrange

$$[[Z]^T[Z]]\{A\} = [Z]^T\{Y\}, \quad [S]\{A\} = [Z]^T\{Y\}$$

### 17.4.2 Solution techniques

The matrix [S] is not diagonally dominant. :. Gauss-Seidel cannot be employed.

- · LU Decomposition
- · Cholesky Method : The matrix [S] is symmetric.
- · Matrix Inverse Approaches: Inefficient.

### 17.4.3 Statistical aspects of least-squares theory

17.5 Nonlinear Regression ( 기생 과 분생).

· Function

$$f(x) = a_0(1 - e^{-a_1 x})$$

; nonlinear → 선형화 불가능 → Iteration Method 사용함.

· Gauss-Newton method

$$y_i = f(x_1; a_0, a_1, \dots, a_m) + e_i$$

$$y_i = f(x_i) + e_i$$

$$f(x_i)$$
 를 Taylor 급수 전개

for two-parameter case

$$f(x_i)_{j+1} = f(x_i)_j + \frac{\partial f(x_i)_j}{\partial a_0} \triangle a_0 + \frac{\partial f(x_i)_j}{\partial a_1} \triangle a_1$$

where 
$$\triangle a_0=a_0^{j+1}-a_0^j$$
 ,  $\triangle a_1=a_1^{j+1}-a_1^j$  j+1; unknowns, j; assumed values

$$y_i - f(x_i)_j = \frac{\partial f(x_i)_j}{\partial a_0} \triangle a_0 + \frac{\partial f(x_i)_j}{\partial a_1} \triangle a_1 + e_i$$

where 
$$f(x_i)$$
: 가정된  $a_0$ ,  $a_1$ 과  $x_i$ 의 함수

$$\{D\} = [Z_i] \{\triangle A\} + \{E\}$$

$$[Z_{j}] = \begin{bmatrix} \frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial a_{0}} & \frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial a_{1}} \\ \frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial a_{0}} & \frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial a_{1}} \\ \vdots & \vdots & \vdots \\ \frac{\partial f_{n}}{\partial a_{0}} & \frac{\partial f_{n}}{\partial a_{1}} \end{bmatrix}, \quad \{D\} = \begin{pmatrix} y - f(x_{1}) \\ y_{2} - f(x_{2}) \\ \vdots \\ y_{n} - f(x_{n}) \end{pmatrix}, \quad \{\triangle A\} = \begin{pmatrix} \triangle a_{0} \\ \triangle a_{1} \end{pmatrix}$$

Applying linear least square theory

$$[[Z_{j}]^{T}[Z_{j}]]\{\triangle A\} = [Z_{j}]^{T}\{D\}$$

$$\begin{cases} a_{0,j+1} = a_{0,j} + \triangle a_{0} \\ a_{1,j+1} = a_{1,j} + \triangle a_{1} \end{cases}$$

Stopping criterion

$$|\varepsilon_a|_k = |\frac{a_{k,j+1} - a_{k,j}}{a_{k,j+1}}| 100\%$$

\* 
$$f(x)$$
 의 미분이 어려운 경우 
$$\frac{\partial f_i}{\partial a_k} \simeq \frac{f(x_i; a_0, \cdots, a_k + h, \cdots, a_m) - f(x_i; a_0, \cdots, a_k, \cdots, a_m)}{h}$$

h: pre-specified small increment

- · Possible shortcoming
- 1. It may converge slowly
- 2. It may oscillate widely, i.e. continually change direction.
- 3. It may not converge at all.

### Chapter 18. Interpolation

· Estimate intermediate value between precise data point most common method : polynomial interpolation

$$f(x) = a_0 + a_1 x + a_2 x^2 + \dots + a_n x^n$$

- For n+1 data point, unique polynomial of order n < Fig. 18.1 >
- Polynomial interpolation
   Determine unique n-th order polynomial for n+1 data
  - · Newton polynomial · Lagrangian polynomial 편리함.

### 18.1 Newton's Divided-Difference Interpolation Polynomials

; the most popular and useful forms

### 18.1.1 Linear interpolation

[ Fig. 18.2 ]
두 점 사이 값 
$$x_0 < x < x_1$$

$$f_1(x) = a_0 + a_1 x$$

$$\begin{cases} f_1(x_0) = a_0 + a_1 x_0 \\ f_1(x_1) = a_0 + a_1 x_1 \end{cases} \rightarrow \begin{cases} a_0 \\ a_1 \end{cases} ?$$

$$\frac{f_1(x) - f_1(x_0)}{x - x_0} = \frac{f_1(x_1) - f_1(x_0)}{x_1 - x_0}$$

· Linear interpolation formula

$$f_1(x) = f(x_0) + \frac{f(x_1) - f(x_0)}{x_1 - x_0} (x - x_0)$$

f(x_1, x_0)}{x_2 - x_0} = \frac{f(x_2, x_1) - f(x_1, x_0)}{x_2 - x_0} = \frac{f(x_2, x_1) - f(x_1, x_0)}{x_2 - x_0} = \frac{f(x_2, x_1) - f(x_1, x_0)}{x_2 - x_0} = \frac{f(x_2,$$

:

$$b_n = f[x_n, x_{n-1}, \dots, x_1, x_0] = \frac{f[x_n, x_{n-1}, \dots, x_2, x_1] - f[x_{n-1}, x_{n-2}, \dots, x_1, x_0]}{x_n - x_0}$$

· First finite divided difference

$$f[x_i, x_j] = \frac{f(x_i) - f(x_j)}{x_i - x_j} \approx f'(x_{i+1/2})$$

· Second finite divided difference

$$f[x_i, x_j, x_k] = \frac{f[x_i, x_j] - f[x_j, x_k]}{x_i - x_k} \approx \frac{1}{2} f''(x_j)$$

· n-th finite divided difference

$$f(x_n, x_{n-1}, \dots, x_1, x_0] = \frac{f(x_n, x_{n-1}, \dots, x_1] - f(x_{n-1}, x_{n-2}, \dots, x_0]}{x_n - x_0} \approx \frac{1}{n!} f^{(n)}(x_{n/2})$$

· Newton's divided-difference interpolating polynomial

$$f_n(x) = f(x_0) + (x - x_0)f[x_1, x_0] + (x - x_0)(x - x_1)f[x_2, x_1, x_0] + \cdots$$

$$+ (x - x_0)(x - x_1)\cdots(x - x_{n-1})f[x_n, x_{n-1}, \cdots, x_0]$$

[ Ex. 18.3 ]

[ Fig. 18.6 ]

### 18.1.4 Error of Newton's Interpolating polynomial

· Taylor's series

$$f = f_0 + f' \triangle x + \frac{1}{2} f'' \triangle x^2 + \dots + \frac{1}{n!} f^{(n)} \triangle x^n + R_n$$

$$R_n = \frac{f^{(n+1)}(\xi)}{(n+1)!} (x_{i+1} - x_i)^{n+1}$$

$$R_n = \frac{f^{(n+1)}(\xi)}{(n+1)!} (x_{i+1} - x_i)^{n+1}$$

$$R_n = \frac{f^{(n+1)}(\xi)}{(n+1)!} (x_{i+1} - x_i)^{n+1}$$

· For n-th order interpolating polynomial

$$R_n = \frac{f^{(n+1)}(\xi)}{(n+1)!} (x - x_0)(x - x_1) \cdots (x - x_n)$$

$$\frac{f^{(n+1)}(x)}{(n+1)!} \approx f[x, x_n, x_{n-1}, \cdots, x_1, x_0]$$

· finite divided difference

$$R_n \approx f[x_{n+1}, x_n, x_{n-1}, \dots, x_0](x - x_0)(x - x_1) \dots (x - x_n)$$
[Ex. 18.4]
$$R_n = f_{n+1}(x) - f_n(x)$$

$$\uparrow \qquad \uparrow \qquad \uparrow \qquad \uparrow \qquad \uparrow \qquad \uparrow \qquad \uparrow \qquad \uparrow \qquad \uparrow \qquad \uparrow \qquad$$

\* higher order interpolating polynomial  $\rightarrow$  highly sensitive to data errors [ Fig. 18.6 ]

### 18.1.5 Computer Algorithm for Newton's Interpolating polynomials

### 18.2 Lagrange Interpolating Polynomials (Newton 性可 唯 班以)

· Avoids the computation of divided difference (되는 제안 되고)
[ Fig 18.10 ] Second order

$$f_{n}(x) = \sum_{i=0}^{n} L_{i}(x) f(x_{i})$$

$$\int_{0}^{n} \frac{x}{x_{i}} dx = \int_{0}^{n} \frac{x - x_{j}}{x_{i} - x_{j}}$$

$$f_{1}(x) = \frac{x - x_{1}}{x_{0} - x_{1}} f(x_{0}) + \frac{x - x_{0}}{x_{1} - x_{0}} f(x_{1}) \qquad (N=1)$$

$$f_{2}(x) = \frac{(x - x_{1})(x - x_{2})}{(x_{0} - x_{1})(x_{0} - x_{2})} f(x_{0}) + \frac{(x - x_{0})(x - x_{2})}{(x_{1} - x_{0})(x_{1} - x_{2})} f(x_{1})$$

$$+ \frac{(x - x_{0})(x - x_{1})}{(x_{2} - x_{0})(x_{2} - x_{1})} f(x_{2}) + (N=2)$$

· Drive from Newton's method

 $f_1(x) = f(x_0) + (x - x_0)f[x_1, x_0]$ 

$$f(x_1, x_0] = \frac{f(x_1)}{x_1 - x_0} + \frac{f(x_0)}{x_0 - x_1} = \frac{f(x_1) - f(x_0)}{x_1 - x_0}$$

$$f_1(x) = f(x_0) + \frac{(x - x_0)}{(x_1 - x_0)} f(x_1) + \frac{(x - x_0)}{(x_0 - x_1)} f(x_0)$$

$$= \frac{(x - x_0)}{(x_1 - x_0)} f(x_1) + \left\{ 1 + \frac{(x - x_0)}{(x_0 - x_1)} \right\} f(x_0)$$

$$f_1(x) = \frac{x - x_1}{x_0 - x_1} f(x_0) + \frac{x - x_0}{x_1 - x_0} f(x_1)$$

· Error

$$R_n = f[x, x_n, x_{n-1}, \dots, x_0] \prod_{i=0}^{n} (x - x_i)$$

Program 상에서 직접 구할 수 없음, 별도 프로그램 필요.

- o Newton's method : 여러 개 x 값에 대하여도 계산가능. program 복잡
- o Lagrange's method ; Program <u>간단</u>, 1개 x 값에 대해서만 계산 가능.

18.4 Inverse Interpolation ( ラカン)

किए के प्रमा गई निकर.

f(x); dependent variables

x; independent variables

conventional interpolation  $(f(x_i), x_i) \rightarrow f(x)$ 

Inverse Interpolation ;

주어진 데이터들  $(f(x_i), x_i)$ 들로부터

주어진 f(x)에 대응되는 x의 값을 찾는 문제.

독립변수와 종속변수를 바꾸어 계산 가능함.

f(x)을 x의 균등한 차이에 대하여 f(x)의 불균등한 차이로 인한 문제 f(x)을 x의 다항식으로 변환 보간 값을 구함.

; 다항식의 근을 구하는 문제가 됨

주어진 자료들의 어떤 구간에 대하여  $f(x) = a_0 + a_1 x + a_2 x^2$ 의 식에서 계수  $a_i$  들을 계산 후식을

$$(-f(x) + a_0) + a_1x + a_2x^2 = 0$$
의 근  $x$ 를 구함.

### 18.6.1 Linear Spline

$$f(x) = f(x_0) + m_0(x - x_0) \quad x_0 \le x \le x_1$$

$$f(x) = f(x_1) + m_1(x - x_1) \quad x_1 \le x \le x_2$$

$$\vdots$$

$$f(x) = f(x_{n-1}) + m_{n-1}(x - x_{n-1}) \quad x_{n-1} \le x \le x_n$$

$$m_i = \frac{f(x_{i+1}) - f(x_i)}{x_{i+1} - x_i} \quad .$$

### 18.6.2 Quadratic Spline

;  $m^{\it th}$  derivative  $\leftarrow (m+1)^{\it th}$  order of polynomial for  $1^{\it st}$  ,  $2^{\it nd}$  derivative 연속  $\leftarrow 3^{\it rd}$  order polynomial

Second order polynomial ; continuous first derivative at the knots  $f_i(x) = a_i x^2 + b_i x + c_i \text{; for each interval between data points}$  for n+1 data points(i=0,1,2,...n); n interval  $\begin{cases} 3 \times n \text{ unknown constants}(a,b,c) \\ \text{Required } 3 \times n \text{ eq. or condition} \end{cases}$ 

1) The function values must be equal at the interior knots  $a_{i-1}x_{i-1}^2+b_{i-1}x_{i-1}+c_{i-1}=f(x_{i-1}) \qquad <\text{Fig. 18.17}$   $a_ix_{i-1}^2+b_ix_{i-1}+c_i=f(x_{i-1})$  for i=2 ,n , condition : 2n-2

2) The first and last function must be pass through the end points

$$a_1x_0^2 + b_1x_0 + c_1 = f(x_0)$$

$$a_n x_n^2 + b_n x_n + c_n = f(x_n)$$

condition: 2

3) The first derivatives at the interior knots must be equal

$$f(x) = 2ax + b$$

$$2a_{i-1}x_{i-1} + b_{i-1} = 2a_ix_{i-1} + b_i$$

i=2,n, condition: n-1

4) Assume that the second derivative is zero at the first point  $a_1 = 0$ 

### 18.6.3 Cubic spline

$$f_i(x) = a_i x^3 + b_i x^2 + c_i x + d_i$$

- 1. The function values must be equal at the interior knots (2n-2)
- 2. The first and last functions must pass through the end points (2)
- 3. The first derivative at the interior knots must be equal (n-1)
- 4. The second derivatives at the interior knots must be equal (n-1)
- 5. The second derivatives at the end knots are zero (2) (+ 4n

or 
$$\frac{\partial^2 f(x_0)}{\partial x^2} = A$$
,  $\frac{\partial^2 f(x_n)}{\partial x^2} = B$ 

Five types of conditions  $\rightarrow$  4n equations  $\rightarrow$  4n unknowns of coefficients

- o (n-1) eqn. 들로부터 → 4n unknowns들을 구할 수 있는 방법은? < Box. 18.3 >
- o More efficient then the quadratic spline

<sup>\*</sup> Second derivative is not continuous.

### \* Derivation of cubic splines

$$f_i(x) = a_i x^3 + b_i x^2 + c_i x + d_i$$
  
 $f'_i(x) = 6a_i x + 2b_i$ 

from first order Laglange interpolating

$$f_{i}^{"}(x) = f_{i}^{"}(x_{i-1}) \frac{x - x_{i}}{x_{i-1} - x_{i}} + f_{i}^{"}(x_{i}) \frac{x - x_{i-1}}{x_{i} - x_{i-1}}$$

적분하면

$$f_{i}'(x) = \frac{f_{i}''(x_{i-1})}{x_{i-1} - x_{i}} \left(\frac{x^{2}}{2} - x_{i}x + C_{1}\right) + \frac{f_{i}''(x_{i})}{x_{i} - x_{i-1}} \left(\frac{x^{2}}{2} - x_{i-1}x + C_{2}\right)$$

다시 적분하면

$$f_{i}(x) = \frac{f_{i}''(x_{i-1})}{x_{i-1} - x_{i}} \left( \frac{x^{3}}{6} - x_{i} \frac{x^{2}}{2} + C_{1} x + D_{1} \right)$$

$$+ \frac{f_{i}''(x_{i})}{x_{i} - x_{i-1}} \left( \frac{x^{3}}{6} - x_{i-1} \frac{x}{2} + C_{2} x + D_{2} \right)$$

$$= \frac{f_{i}''(x_{i-1})}{6(x_{i-1} - x_{i})} \left\{ (x - x_{i})^{3} + (C_{1} - 3x_{i}^{2}) x + (D_{1} + x_{i}^{3}) \right\}$$

$$+ \frac{f_{i}''(x_{i})}{6(x_{i} - x_{i-1})} \left\{ (x - x_{i-1})^{3} + (C_{2} - 3x_{i-1}^{2}) x + (D_{2} + x_{i-1}^{3}) \right\}$$

 $C_1$ ,  $C_2$ ,  $D_1$ ,  $D_2$  는 임의 상수 이므로 정리하면

$$f_i(x) = \frac{f_i''(x_{i-1})}{6(x_i - x_{i-1})} (x_i - x)^3 + \frac{f_i''(x_i)}{6(x_i - x_{i-1})} (x - x_{i-1})^3 + A(x_i - x) + B(x - x_{i-1})$$

 $x = x_i$  에서  $f_i(x_i)$  이므로

$$f_i(x_i) = \frac{f_i''(x_i)}{6(x_i - x_{i-1})} (x_i - x_{i-1})^3 + B(x_i - x_{i-1})$$
  

$$\therefore B = \frac{f_i(x_i)}{(x_i - x_{i-1})} - \frac{f_i''(x_i)}{6} (x_i - x_{i-1})$$

 $x = x_{i-1}$  에서  $f_i(x_{i-1})$  이므로

$$f_i(x_{i-1}) = \frac{f_i''(x_{i-1})}{6(x_i - x_{i-1})} (x_i - x_{i-1})^3 + A(x_i - x_{i-1})$$
  
$$\therefore A = \frac{f_i(x_{i-1})}{(x_i - x_{i-1})} - \frac{f_i''(x_{i-1})}{6} (x_i - x_{i-1})$$

A, B를 대입하면

$$f_{i}(x) = \frac{f_{i}''(x_{i-1})}{6(x_{i}-x_{i-1})} (x_{i}-x)^{3} + \frac{f_{i}''(x_{i})}{6(x_{i}-x_{i-1})} (x_{i}-x_{i-1})^{3} + \left[\frac{f(x_{i-1})}{x_{i}-x_{i-1}} - \frac{f''(x_{i-1})(x_{i}-x_{i-1})}{6}\right](x_{i}-x) + \left[\frac{f(x_{i})}{x_{i}-x_{i-1}} - \frac{f''(x_{i})(x_{i}-x_{i-1})}{6}\right](x_{i}-x_{i-1})$$

where unknowns are  $f''(x_{i-1})$  and  $f''(x_i)$ 

윗식을 미분하고  $f_{i+1}'(x_i) = f_i'(x_i)$  인 조건을 이용하면,

$$f_{i}'(x) = \frac{-f_{i}''(x_{i-1})}{2(x_{i}-x_{i-1})} (x_{i}-x)^{2} + \frac{f_{i}''(x_{i})}{2(x_{i}-x_{i-1})} (x-x_{i-1})^{2}$$

$$-\left[\frac{f(x_{i-1})}{x_{i}-x_{i-1}} - \frac{f''(x_{i-1})(x_{i}-x_{i-1})}{6}\right]$$

$$+\left[\frac{f(x_{i})}{x_{i}-x_{i-1}} - \frac{f''(x_{i})(x_{i}-x_{i-1})}{6}\right]$$

$$f_{i}'(x_{i}) = \frac{f_{i}''(x_{i})}{2} (x_{i}-x_{i-1}) - \frac{f(x_{i-1})}{x_{i}-x_{i-1}} - \frac{f''(x_{i-1})(x_{i}-x_{i-1})}{6}$$

$$+\frac{f(x_{i})}{x_{i}-x_{i-1}} - \frac{f''(x_{i})(x_{i}-x_{i-1})}{6}$$

$$= f_{i+1}'(x_{i}) = -\frac{f_{i}''(x_{i})}{2} (x_{i+1}-x_{i}) - \frac{f(x_{i})}{x_{i+1}-x_{i}} - \frac{f''(x_{i})(x_{i+1}-x_{i})}{6}$$

$$+\frac{f(x_{i+1})}{x_{i+1}-x_{i}} - \frac{f''(x_{i+1})(x_{i+1}-x_{i})}{6}$$

정리하면

$$(x_{i}-x_{i-1})f''(x_{i-1}) + 2(x_{i+1}-x_{i-1})f''(x_{i}) + (x_{i+1}-x_{i})f''(x_{i+1})$$

$$= \frac{6}{(x_{i+1}-x_{i})}[f(x_{i+1})-f(x_{i})] + \frac{6}{(x_{i}-x_{i-1})}[f(x_{i-1})-f(x_{i})]$$

$$= RHS$$

, for  $i = 1, 2, \dots, n-1$

### PT6.2 Mathematical Background

### Chapter 21. Newton-Cotes Integration Formulas

The most common numerical integration schemes.

$$I = \int_{a}^{b} f(x)dx \simeq \int_{a}^{b} f_{n}(x)dx$$

where 
$$f_n(x) = a_0 + a_1 + \dots + a_{n-1}x^{n-1} + a_nx^n$$

; A complicated function or tabulated data  $\rightarrow$ 

Some approximating function that being to integration.

[Fig. 21.1, Fig. 21.2]

- · Closed form; The data points at the beginning and end of limits of integration.
- Open form ; The integration limits are extended beyond the range of the data

: similar to extrapolation [Fig. 21.3 a), b)]

- ; not generally used for definite integration.
- ; utilized for evaluating improper integration and for solution of ODE.

### 21.1 The trapezoidal rule (소리가 서울당).

· First degree of N-C formula

$$I = \int_{a}^{b} f(x)dx \simeq \int_{a}^{b} f_{1}(x)dx$$

$$f_1(x) = f(a) + \frac{f(b) - f(a)}{b - a}(x - a)$$
; Linear interpolation

Form [Box 21.1]

$$I = \int_{a}^{b} [f(a) + \frac{f(b) - f(a)}{b - a} (x - a)] dx$$

$$= \left[ f(a)x + \frac{f(b) - f(a)}{b - a} \frac{1}{2} (x^{2} - 2ax) \right]_{a}^{b}$$

$$= \left[ f(a)(b - a) + \frac{f(b) - f(a)}{b - a} \frac{1}{2} \{ (b - a)^{2} - 2a(b - a) \} \right]$$

$$= \left[ f(a)(b - a) + \{ f(b) - f(a) \} \frac{1}{2} (b - a) \right]$$

$$= \left[ f(a)(b - a) + \frac{1}{2} (b - a) f(b) - \frac{1}{2} (b - a) f(a) \right]$$

$$\therefore I = (b - a) \frac{f(a) + f(b)}{2}$$

[ Fig. 21.4, 21.5 ] ;  $I \approx \text{width} \times \text{average height}$ 

### 21.1.1 Error of the trapezoidal rule

· Newtons interpolating formula (equal space)

$$f_n(x) = b_0 + b_1(x - x_0) + \dots + b_n(x - x_0)(x - x_1) \dots (x - x_{n-1})$$

$$b_0 = f(x_0), \ b_1 = f[x_1, x_0] = \frac{f(x_1) - f(x_0)}{x_1 - x_0}$$

$$b_2 = f[x_2, x_1, x_0] = \frac{\frac{f(x_2) - f(x_0)}{x_2 - x_0} - \frac{f(x_1) - f(x_0)}{x_1 - x_0}}{\frac{x_2 - x_1}{x_0}}, \dots, b_n = f[x_n, x_{n-1}, \dots, x_1, x_0]$$

$$f(x_n, x_{n-1}, \dots, x_1, x_0] = \frac{f(x_n, x_{n-1}, \dots, x_1] - f(x_{n-1}, x_{n-2}, \dots, x_0]}{x_n - x_0} \approx n! \ f^{(n)}(x_{n/2})$$

· Newton's divided-difference interpolating polynomial

$$f_n(x) = f(x_0) + (x - x_0)f(x_1, x_0] + (x - x_0)(x - x_1)f(x_2, x_1, x_0] + \cdots + (x - x_0)(x - x_1)\cdots(x - x_{n-1})f(x_n, x_{n-1}, \dots, x_0]$$

$$f_n(x) = f(x_0) + \frac{\triangle f(x_0)}{h} (x - x_0) + \frac{\triangle^2 f(x_0)}{2! h^2} (x - x_0) (x - x_0 - h)$$

$$+ \frac{\triangle^3 f(x_0)}{3! h^3} (x - x_0) (x - x_0 - h) (x - x_0 - 2h) + \cdots$$

$$+ \frac{\triangle^n f(x_0)}{n! h^n} (x - x_0) (x - x_0 - h) \cdots (x - x_0 - (n-1)h) + R_n$$

$$\triangle^n f(x_0)/n! h^n = f[x_0 x_1 \cdots, x_n]$$

### [ Box. 21.2 ]

· Newton-Gregory forward formula

$$\alpha = \frac{x - x_0}{h}$$

$$x - x_0 = \alpha h$$

$$x - x_0 - h = \alpha h - h = h(\alpha - 1)$$

:

$$x - x_0 - (n-1)h = \alpha h - (n-1)h = h(\alpha - n + 1)$$

$$f_n(x) = f(x_0) + \triangle f(x_0) \alpha + \frac{\triangle^2 f(x_0)}{2!} \alpha(\alpha - 1) + \cdots + \frac{\triangle^n f(x_0)}{n!} \alpha(\alpha - 1) \cdots (\alpha - n + 1) + R_n$$

where

$$R_{n} = \frac{f^{(n+1)}(\xi)}{(n+1)!} h^{n+1} \alpha (\alpha - 1) (\alpha - 2) \cdots$$

: for first order scheme

$$I = \int_{a}^{b} [f(a) + \triangle f(a)\alpha + \frac{f''(\xi)}{2} \alpha(\alpha - 1)h^{2}] dx$$

$$dx = h d\alpha \quad \leftarrow \quad \alpha h = x - x_{0}$$

$$I = h \int_{a}^{b} [f(a) + \triangle f(a)\alpha + \frac{f''(\xi)}{2} \alpha(\alpha - 1)h^{2}] d\alpha$$

$$I = h [\alpha f(a) + \frac{\alpha^{2}}{2} \triangle f(a) + (\frac{\alpha^{3}}{6} - \frac{\alpha^{2}}{4})f''(\xi)h^{2}]_{0}^{1}$$

$$I = h [f(a) + \frac{\triangle f(a)}{2}] - \frac{1}{12} f''(\xi)h^{3}$$

$$\therefore \quad I = h \frac{f(a) + f(b)}{2} - \frac{1}{12} f''(\xi)h^{3}$$

$$\cdot \quad \text{Error} \quad ; \quad E_{r} = -\frac{1}{12} f''(\xi)(b - a)^{3}$$

### 21.1.2 The multiple-application(Composite) trapezoidal rule

 $a \sim b$  사이를 n개로 나눔, f(x); known function

$$h = \frac{b-a}{n}$$

$$I = \int_{x_0}^{x_1} f(x) dx + \int_{x_1}^{x_2} f(x) dx + \dots + \int_{x_{n-1}}^{x_n} f(x) dx$$

$$I \simeq h \frac{f(x_0) + f(x_1)}{2} + h \frac{f(x_1) + f(x_2)}{2} + \dots + h \frac{f(x_{n-1}) + f(x_n)}{2}$$

$$I \simeq \frac{h}{2} \Big[ f(x_0) + 2 \sum_{i=1}^{n-1} f(x_i) + f(x_n) \Big], \qquad h = \frac{b-a}{n}$$

$$I = (\underbrace{b-a}) \frac{f(x_0) + 2\sum_{i=1}^{n-1} f(x_i) + f(x_n)}{2n} = \text{width} \times \text{average height}$$

$$\cdot \text{Error} : E_t = -\frac{(b-a)^3}{12n^3} \sum_{i=1}^{n-1} f''(\xi_i)$$

$$\overline{f''} \simeq \frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n} f''(\xi_i)}{n}$$
; Mean value of the 2nd derivative.

$$\sum f''(\xi_i) \simeq n \overline{f''}, \quad \xi_a = -\frac{(b-a)^3}{12n^2} \overline{f''}$$

### 22.2 Romberg Integration

· At large values of n, the error starts to increase as round-off errors begin to dominate.  $\rightarrow$  [ Fig 22.2 ]

22.2.1 Richardson's extrapolation: 3<sup>rd</sup> or more accurate approximation.

· Error correction techniques

$$I = I(h) + E(h)$$

I: exact value

I(h): approximate value(trapezoidal value), n segments,

h = (b - a)/n :step size

E(h): truncation error

For two separate estimates using step sizes of  $h_1$  and  $h_2$ .

$$I(h_1) + E(h_1) = I(h_2) + E(h_2)$$

from trapezoidal rule

$$E \simeq -\frac{b-a}{12} h^2 \overline{f^{\prime\prime}}$$

If  $\overline{f''}$  is constant

$$\frac{E(h_1)}{E(h_2)} \simeq \frac{h_1^2}{h_2^2}$$
,  $E(h_1) \simeq E(h_2) \frac{h_1^2}{h_2^2}$

· Forward difference

$$f'(x_i) = \frac{f(x_{i+1}) - f(x_i)}{h} - \frac{f''(x_i)}{2}h + O(h^2)$$

$$f'(x_i) = \frac{f(x_{i+1}) - f(x_i)}{h} + O(h)$$

$$f''(x_i) = \frac{f(x_{i+2}) - 2f(x_{i+1}) + f(x_i)}{h^2} + O(h)$$

· Backward difference

$$f'(x_i) = \frac{f(x_i) - f(x_{i-1})}{h} + O(h)$$

$$f''(x_i) = \frac{f(x_i) - 2f(x_{i-1}) + f(x_{i-2})}{h^2} + O(h)$$
[ Fig 23.2 ]

· Centered difference

$$f'(x_i) = \frac{f(x_{i+1}) - f(x_{i-1})}{2h} + O(h^2)$$

$$f''(x_i) = \frac{f(x_{i+1}) - 2f(x_i) + f(x_{i-1})}{h^2} + O(h^2)$$
[Fig 23.3]

### 23.2 Richardson Extrapolation

To improve derivative estimates

- 1) decrease the step size
- 2) use a higher order formula that employs more point
- 3) Richardson extrapolation

Richardson extrapolation integration formular

$$I \simeq I(h_2) + \left[\frac{1}{(h_1/h_2)^2 - 1}\right] [I(h_2) - I(h_1)], \quad \text{Error} \quad O(h^4)$$

When 
$$h_2 = h_1/2$$

$$I \simeq \frac{4}{3} I(h_2) - \frac{1}{3} I(h_1)$$

In a similar fashion

$$D \simeq \frac{4}{3} D(h_2) - \frac{1}{3} D(h_1)$$
, Error  $O(h^4)$ 

[Ex. 23.2]

$$D(0.5) = -1.0$$
,  $\varepsilon_t = -9.6\%$ 

$$D(0.25) = -0.934375$$
,  $\varepsilon_t = -2.4\%$ 

$$D = \frac{4}{3}(-0.93437 \frac{5)-1}{3(-1.0)} = -0.9125$$
, a perfect result.

### 23.3 Derivatives of Unequally Spaced Data

Finite divided difference - had to be evenly spaced unequal interval data - empirically derived information

Use a second order Lagrange interpolating polynomial

The second order polynomial can be differentiated analytically to give

$$f'(x) = f(x_{i-1}) \frac{2x - x_i - x_{i+1}}{(x_{i-1} - x_i)(x_{i-1} - x_{i+1})} + f(x_i) \frac{2x - x_{i-1} - x_{i+1}}{(x_i - x_{i-1})(x_i - x_{i+1})} + f(x_{i+1}) \frac{2x - x_{i-1} - x_i}{(x_{i+1} - x_i)(x_{i+1} - x_{i-1})}$$

### 23.4 Derivatives and Integrals for Data with Errors

Empirical data is that it usually includes measurement error.

Numerical differentiation; amplify errors

[Fig. 23.5]

to use least squares regression to fit a smooth

### 23.4.1 Differentiation versus Integration of Uncertain Data

Curve fitting techniques can be used.

Differentiation; Amplify errors

Integration; very forgiving for Uncertain Data

Extrapolation; Euler's Method

25.1 Euler's Method (并经子名).

(Euler-Cauchy or point-slope method)  $\frac{dy}{dx} = \phi = f(x_i, y_i) \text{ given function or value}$ finite difference;  $\frac{dy}{dx} \simeq \frac{y_{i+1} - y_i}{h} \simeq f(x_i, y_i)$   $y_{i+1} = y_i + f(x_i, y_i)h \text{ linearly extrapolation, } h \text{ step size}$  [ Ex. 25.1 ] [ Table 251 ]

### 25.1.1 Error analysis for Euler method

- 1. Truncation or descretization error; nature of techniques
- 2. Round-off error; retained by a computer(유효숫자의 한계)

Magnitude and properties of T.E.

$$-y' = f(x, y)$$

· Taylor series

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + y_i'h + \frac{y_i''}{2}h^2 + \dots + \frac{y_i^{(n)}}{n!}h^n + R_n$$
, where  $R_n = \frac{Y^{(n+1)}(\xi)}{(n+1)!}h^{n+1}$ 

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + f(x_i, y_i)h + \underbrace{\frac{f'(x_i, y_i)}{2}h^2 + \dots + \frac{f^{(n-1)}(x_i, y_i)}{n!}h^n + O(h^{n+1})}_{\text{T.E. of Euler's method}}$$

$$E_a = O(h^2)$$

- 1) Taylor series only provides an estimate of the local truncation error. In actual problems, functions are more complicated than simple polynomials.
- 2) Derivative not always easy to obtain

To evaluate the Taylor series expansion  $\rightarrow$  T.E. of f'(x);  $2^{nd}$  order of h T.E. of f(x);  $1^{st}$  order of h

- $\Rightarrow$  1. T.E. proportional to step size.  $\lim_{k\to 0} T.E. = 0$ 
  - 2. linear function  $\rightarrow$   $T.E. \approx 0$  [Fig. 25.5] From A 1st ada Telles technique
- 25.1.2 Algorithm for Euler's Method

[ Fig. 25.6 ], [ Fig 25.7 ]

25.1.3 Higher-order Taylor series method.

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + f(x_i, y_i)h + \frac{f'(x_i, y_i)}{2}h^2, \quad E_a = \frac{f''(x_i, y_i)}{6}h^3$$

$$f'(x, y) = \frac{\partial f(x, y)}{\partial x} + \frac{\partial f(x, y)}{\partial y}\frac{dy}{dx}$$

$$f''(x, y) = \frac{\partial (\partial f/\partial x) + (\partial f/\partial y)(dy/dx)}{\partial x} + \frac{\partial (\partial f/\partial x) + (\partial f/\partial y)(dy/dx)}{\partial y}\frac{dy}{dx}$$

- · Need higher derivative -> become more complicated
- 25.2 Improvements of Euler's Method Runge-Kutta methods
- 25.2.1 Heun's method; Predictor Corrector approach; multistep method

To improve the estimate of the slope; [Fig. 25.9]

• Predictor

$$y_{i}' = f(x_{i}, y_{i})$$

$$y_{i+1}^{0} = y_{i} + f(x_{i}, y_{i})h$$

$$y_{i+1}' = f(x_{i+1}, y_{i+1}^{0})$$

· Average slope 
$$(x_i, y_i)$$
  $= \frac{y_i' + y_{i+1}'}{2} = \frac{f(x_i, y_i) + f(x_{i+1}, y_{i+1}^0)}{2}$ 

· Corrector

Corrector 
$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \frac{f(x_i, y_i) + f(x_{i+1}, y_{i+1}^0)}{2}h$$
 Corrector Garage Tenation 9 %.

· Iterative method

$$\Rightarrow y_{i+1}^0 \rightarrow y_{i+1}^j$$
;  $j$ : iteration No.

$$|\varepsilon_a| = \left| \frac{y_{i+1}^j - y_{i+1}^{j-1}}{y_{i+1}^j} \right| \times 100\%$$

· Solely a function of independent variables, the predictor step is not required iteration

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \frac{f(x_i) + f(x_{i+1})}{2}h$$

· Comparison with Trapezoidal rule

$$\frac{dy}{dx} = f(x)$$

$$\int_{y_{i}}^{y_{i+1}} dy = \int_{x_{i}}^{x_{i+1}} f(x) dx$$

$$y_{i+1} - y_{i} = \int_{x_{i}}^{x_{i+1}} f(x) dx$$

$$\int_{a}^{b} f(x) dx \approx (b - a) \frac{f(a) + f(b)}{2}$$

$$\int_{x_{i}}^{x_{i+1}} f(x) dx \approx \frac{f(x_{i}) + f(x_{i+1})}{2} h$$

$$\therefore y_{i+1} = y_{i} + \frac{f(x_{i}) + f(x_{i+1})}{2} h$$

$$E_{t} = -\frac{f''(\xi)}{12} h^{3} \quad [\text{ Fig. 25.11 }]$$

### 25.2.2 The Midpoint (or improved Polygon) method(Modified Euler)

[ Fig. 25.12 ]

 $y_{i+1/2} = y_i + f(x_i, y_i) \frac{h}{2}$ ; Value of y at the midpoint of the interval.

 $y_{i+1/2} = f(x_{i+1/2}, y_{i+1/2})$ ; Slope at the midpoint of the interval

 $y_{i+1} = y_i + f(x_{i+1/2}, y_{i+1/2})h$ ; extrapolation linearly from  $x_i$  to  $x_{i+1}$ 

· Can not be applied iteratively

cf. centered finite divided differences

Local T.E. =  $O(h^3)$ , Global T.E. =  $O(h^2)$ 

### 25.2.3 Computer Algorithm

[ Fig. 25.13 ]

### 25.3 Runge-Kutta Methods

· General form

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \phi(x_i, y_i, h)h$$

 $\phi(x_i, y_i, h)$ : increment function 2 明显 地址 地址 中间 representative slope over the interval

$$\phi = a_1 k_1 + a_2 k_2 + \dots + a_n k_n$$

a: constant , k= 1/2/1

Efficient Computation

$$\begin{array}{c}
(k_{1}) = f(x_{i}, y_{i}) \\
(k_{2}) = f(x_{i} + p_{1}h, y_{i} + q_{11}k_{1}h) \\
k_{3} = f(x_{i} + p_{2}h, y_{i} + q_{21}k_{1}h + q_{22}k_{2}h) \\
\vdots \\
k_{n} = f(x_{i} + p_{n-1}h, y_{i} + q_{n-1,1}k_{1}h + q_{n-1,2}k_{2}h + \dots + q_{n-1,n-1}k_{n-1}h)
\end{array}$$

- $\cdot$  n=1 Euler method
- · n choice, a,p,q find from Taylor's series

k's; recurrence relationships: efficient for computer calculations.

### 25.3.1 Second-Order Runge-Kutta methods

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + (a_1k_1 + a_2k_2)h$$

where

$$k_1 = f(x_i, y_i)$$

$$k_2 = f(x_i + p_1 h, y_i + q_{11} k_1 h)$$

### [ Box 25.1 ]

 $y_{i+1} \approx y_i + f(x_i, y_i)h + f'(x_i, y_i)h^2/2$ ; second order Taylor series

$$f'(x_i, y_i) = \frac{\partial f}{\partial x} + \frac{\partial f}{\partial y} \frac{\partial y}{\partial x} = \frac{\partial f}{\partial x} + f(x, y) \frac{\partial f}{\partial y}$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + f(x_i, y_i)h + \left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x} + \frac{\partial f}{\partial y} \frac{\partial y}{\partial x}\right) \frac{h^2}{2} \qquad ----$$

Taylor series for a two-variable function

$$g(x+r, y+s) = g(x,y) + r\frac{\partial g}{\partial x} + s\frac{\partial g}{\partial y} + \cdots$$

$$k_2 = f(x_i + p_1 h, y_i + q_{11} k_1 h) = f(x_i, y_i) + p_1 h \frac{\partial f}{\partial x} + q_{11} k_1 h \frac{\partial f}{\partial y} + O(h^2)$$

· Heun method with a single corrector ( $a_2 = 1/2$ )

$$a_1 = 1/2$$
,  $P_1 = q_{11} = 1$ 

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + (\frac{1}{2}k_1 + \frac{1}{2}k_2)h$$

where

$$k_1 = f(x_i, y_i)$$

$$k_2 = f(x_i + h, y_i + hk_1)$$

· Improved poligon method ( $a_2 = 1$ )

$$a_1 = 0$$
,  $P_1 = q_{11} = 1/2$ 

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + k_2 h$$

where

$$k_1 = f(x_i, y_i)$$

$$k_2 = f(x_i + \frac{1}{2}h, y_i + \frac{1}{2}hk_1)$$

· Ralston's method  $(a_2=2/3)$ 

$$a_1 = 1/3$$
,  $P_1 = q_{11} = 3/4$ 

$$\hat{y}_{i+1} = y_i + (\frac{1}{3}k_1 + \frac{2}{3}k_2)h$$

where

$$k_1 = f(x_i, y_i)$$

$$k_2 = f(x_i + \frac{3}{4}h, y_i + \frac{3}{4}hk_1)$$
 < Fig.19.14 >

### 25.3.2 Third-order Runge-Kutta methods

6 eqn.s and 8 unknowns

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \left[\frac{1}{6}(k_1 + 4k_2 + k_3)\right]h$$
; Simpson's 1/3 rule

local T.E. 
$$O(h^4)$$
, Groval T.E.  $O(h^3)$ 

where

$$k_1 = f(x_i, y_i)$$

$$k_2 = f(x_i + \frac{1}{2}h, y_i + \frac{1}{2}hk_1)$$

$$k_3 = f(x_i + h, y_i - hk_1 + 2hk_2)$$

### 25.3.3 Fourth-order Runge-Kutta methods

· n=4, most popular, classical RK method

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \left[\frac{1}{6}(k_1 + 2k_2 + 2k_3 + k_4)\right]h$$

where

$$k_1 = f(x_i, y_i)$$

$$k_2 = f(x_i + \frac{1}{2}h, y_i + \frac{1}{2}hk_1)$$

$$k_3 = f(x_i + \frac{1}{2}h, y_i + \frac{1}{2}hk_2)$$
  $k_4 = f(x_i + h, y_i + hk_3)$ 

$$k_4 = f(x_i + h, y_i + hk_3)$$

: Simpson's 1/3 rule

### 25.3.4 Higher-order Runge-Kutta methods

• n=5, Butcher's fifth-order RK method

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \left[\frac{1}{90}(7k_1 + 32k_3 + 12k_4 + 32k_5 + 7k_6)\right]h$$

where

$$k_1 = f(x_i, y_i),$$

$$k_2 = f(x_i + \frac{1}{4}h, y_i + \frac{1}{4}hk_1)$$

$$k_3 = f(x_i + \frac{1}{4}h, y_i + \frac{1}{8}hk_1 + \frac{1}{8}hk_2)$$
  $k_4 = f(x_i + \frac{1}{2}h, y_i - \frac{1}{2}hk_2 + hk_3)$ 

$$k_4 = f(x_i + \frac{1}{2}h, y_i - \frac{1}{2}hk_2 + hk_3)$$

$$k_5 = f(x_i + \frac{3}{4}h, y_i + \frac{3}{16}hk_1 + \frac{9}{16}hk_4)$$

$$k_6 = f(x_i + h, y_i - \frac{3}{7}hk_1 + \frac{2}{7}hk_2 + \frac{12}{7}hk_3 - \frac{12}{7}hk_4 + \frac{8}{7}hk_5)$$

 ;  $n_f$ : the order of method

### 25.4.2 Runge-Kutta Method

$$\begin{cases} \frac{dy_1}{dx} = f_1(x, y_1, y_2) \\ \frac{dy_2}{dx} = f_2(x, y_1, y_2) \end{cases}$$

$$\begin{cases} y_{1,i+1} = y_{1,i} + \left[\frac{1}{6}(k_{1,1} + 2k_{2,1} + 2k_{3,1} + k_{4,1})\right]h \\ y_{2,i+1} = y_{2,i} + \left[\frac{1}{6}(k_{1,2} + 2k_{2,2} + 2k_{3,2} + k_{4,2})\right]h \end{cases}$$

where

$$\begin{cases} k_{1,1} = f_1(x_i, y_{1,i}, y_{2,i}) \\ k_{1,2} = f_2(x_i, y_{1,i}, y_{2,i}) \end{cases} \begin{cases} k_{2,1} = f_1(x_i + \frac{1}{2}h, y_{1,i} + \frac{1}{2}hk_{1,1}, y_{2,i} + \frac{1}{2}hk_{1,2}) \\ k_{2,2} = f_2(x_i + \frac{1}{2}h, y_{1,i} + \frac{1}{2}hk_{1,1}, y_{2,i} + \frac{1}{2}hk_{1,2}) \end{cases}$$

$$\begin{cases} k_{3,1} = f_1(x_i + \frac{1}{2}h, y_{1,i} + \frac{1}{2}hk_{1,2}, y_{2,i} + \frac{1}{2}hk_{2,2}) \\ k_{3,2} = f_2(x_i + \frac{1}{2}h, y_{1,i} + \frac{1}{2}hk_{1,2}, y_{2,i} + \frac{1}{2}hk_{2,2}) \end{cases}$$

$$\begin{cases} k_{4,1} = f_1(x_i + h, y_{1,i} + hk_{3,1}, y_{2,i} + hk_{3,2}) \\ k_{4,2} = f_2(x_i + h, y_{1,i} + hk_{3,1}, y_{2,i} + hk_{3,2}) \end{cases}$$

### 25.5 Adaptive Runge-Kutta Methods

Const. step-size → abrupt change prob. [Fig. 25.20] ; very small step size would be required

· Automatically adjust the step size

→ Adaptive step size control

$$\frac{dy}{dx} = f(x, y)$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + f(x, y) h + \frac{1}{2} f'(x, y) h^2 + \cdots$$

f'(x, y); very large value  $\rightarrow$  error estimate에 사용

- · Truncation error between different-order RK method
- · Truncation error between same-order RK method with different step size  $\Rightarrow$  Local T.E. estimate

### 25.5.1 Adaptive RK or Step-Having Method

· Single step prediction, two-half step

 $y_1$ : single step prediction,  $y_2$ : 2개의 1/2 step prediction.

The error;  $\triangle = y_2 - y_1$ 

For the  $4^{th}$  order RK, correct the results.

$$y_2 \leftarrow y_2 + \frac{\Delta}{15}$$
; 5<sup>th</sup> order accurate;  $O(h^5)$ 

### 25.5.2 RK Fehlberg

Step controling;  $\triangle = y_2 - y_1$ 

$$y_2$$
;  $5^{th}$  order  $RK$ :  $k_1 \sim k_6$ ,  $y_1$ ;  $4^{th}$  order  $RK$ :  $k_1 \sim k_4$ 

(10 functions evaluation)/step  $\rightarrow$  (5<sup>th</sup> order RK  $k_i$ ) = (4<sup>th</sup> order RK  $k_i$ ) 라면 only 6 f(x, y) evaluation / step

Eqn. 25.45: 4<sup>th</sup> order RK

Eqn. 25.46 :  $5^{th}$  order RK  $\rightarrow$  error = eqn.25.46 - eqn.25.45

; Cash-Karp RK method

### 25.5.3 Step size control

· Increase the step size if the error is too small and Decrease it if the error is too large.

$$h_{new} = h_{present} \left| \frac{\triangle_{new}}{\triangle_{present}} \right|^{\alpha}$$

 $\triangle_{\mathit{present}}$  : present accuracy(  $y_2 - y_1$  ),  $\triangle_{\mathit{new}}$  : desired accuracy

a: 0.2 when  $\triangle_{present} \leq \triangle_{new}$ ; step size increase

0.25 when  $\triangle_{present} \rangle \triangle_{new}$ ; step size decrease

When  $y \approx 0$  or  $\triangle_{present} \approx 0$ ,  $\varepsilon$ ; overall tolerence level; prescribed value

$$\triangle_{new} = \varepsilon y_{scale}$$
 where  $y_{scale} = |y| + \left| h \frac{dy}{dx} \right|$

### Chapter 26. Stiffness and Multistep Method

stiff ODEs - 빠른 변화와 느린 변화를 보이는 항들을 동시에 갖는 ODEs Implicit solution.

One-step Method;  $y_{i+1}$  at  $x_{i+1} \leftarrow (x_i, y_i, h_i)$ 

Multi-step Method;

### 26.1 Stiffness

Stiff system; 빠른 변화와 느린 변화를 보이는 항들을 동시에 갖는 ODEs 급격한 변화 성분 - 급격히 사라져버림으로 결과에 큰 영향 없음. 느린 변화 성분 - 해에 중요한 영향 미침.

[Ex.] a single stiff ODE

$$\frac{dy}{dt} = -1000y + 3000 - 2000e^{-t}$$

y(0) = 0라면 해석적인 해는 다음과 같다.

$$y = 3 - 0.998e^{-1000t} - 2.002e^{-t}$$

여기서, 시간이 증가해감에 따라  $(0.998e^{-1000t})$  항은 급격히 소멸되어 t=0.005 이후에는 y에 영향을 미치지 못함.

이러한 경우 해의 안정성(stability)을 위하여 step size의 제한이 있다.

이들을 알아보기 위하여 방정식의 homogeneous part의 경향을 검토한다.

$$\frac{dy}{dt} = -ay$$

y(0) = 0라면 그 해는

$$y = y_0 e^{-at}$$

이 되어 시간이 지남에 따라 서서히 0에 접근하여 간다.

Euler's method를 이용한 수치 해는

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \frac{dy_i}{dt} h = y_i - ay_i h$$

또는  $y_{i+1} = y_i(1-ah)$ 

이식의 안정성의 문제는 분명히 step size h에 종속되어.

$$|1 - ah| < 1.0$$

이 되어야 한다.

위의 예제에서

$$\frac{dy}{dt} = -1000y + 3000 - 2000e^{-t}$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \frac{dy_i}{dt} h = y_i + (-1000y_i + 3000 - 2000e^{-ih})h$$
$$= y_i(1 - 1000h) + (3000 - 2000e^{-ih})h$$

에서 |1-1000h|<1.0 이기 위해서는 h<0.002이어야 해는  $i\to\infty$  일 때 안정하

다.

안정성의 문제와 더불어 이때에는 이보다 정확도를 위해 더 적은 step size을 택하여야 한다. 따라서 step size가 작아짐으로 계산시간이 많이 필요하고 round off error도 증가한다.

급격한 변화를 갖는 성분은 아주 짧은 동안 해에 영향을 미치지만 해의 안정 성과 정확성에 매우 큰 영향을 준다.

해결 방법: step size control(adaptive step size) 사용. - stability 문제 상존.; explicit approaches

### Implicit method

다음과 같이 dy/dt을 다음 단계의 값 이용하여

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \frac{dy_{i+1}}{dt} h = y_i - ay_{i+1}h$$
$$y_{i+1} = \frac{y_i}{(1+ah)}$$

이 경우 어떠한 step size h를 사용하여도  $(i \to \infty)$ 일 경우  $(y_{i+1} < y_i)$  이되어 안정된 해를 구할 수 있다.

이러한 경우 unconditionally stable하다라고 한다.

### [EX. 26.1]

h=0.0015, h=0.0005 및 h=0.05로 implicit method 결과. [Fig. 26.2]

Systems of ODEs(stiff)

$$\begin{cases} \frac{dy_1}{dt} = -5y_1 + 3y_2 \\ \frac{dy_2}{dt} = 100y_1 - 301y_2 \end{cases}$$

$$\begin{cases} y_{1,i+1} = y_{1,i} + \frac{dy_{1,i}}{dt} h = y_{1,i} + (-5y_{1,i} + 3y_{2,i})h = y_{1,i}(1 - 5h) + 3y_{2,i}h \\ y_{2,i+1} = y_{2,i} + \frac{dy_{2,i}}{dt} h = y_{2,i} + (100y_{1,i} - 301y_{2,i})h = y_{2,i}(1 - 301h) + 100y_{1,i}h \end{cases}$$
안정성을 위하여  $h < \frac{2}{301}$ 이어야 함.

Implicit method;

$$\begin{cases} y_{1,i+1} = y_{1,i} + \frac{dy_{1,i+1}}{dt} h = y_{1,i} + (-5y_{1,i+1} + 3y_{2,i+1})h \\ y_{2,i+1} = y_{2,i} + \frac{dy_{2,i+1}}{dt} h = y_{2,i} + (100y_{1,i+1} - 301y_{2,i+1})h \end{cases}$$

$$\begin{cases} (1+5h) y_{1,i+1} - 3h y_{2,i+1} = y_{1,i} \\ -100 h y_{1,i+1} + (1+301h)y_{2,i+1} = y_{2,i} \end{cases}$$

$$\begin{bmatrix} (1+5h) & -3h \\ -100 & h & (1+301 & h) \end{bmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} y_{1,i+1} \\ y_{2,i+1} \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} y_{1,i} \\ y_{2,i} \end{pmatrix}$$

; unconditionally stable

Implicit Euler's method; unconditionally stable, first order accuracy 기타 여러 가지 Implicit method들이 있음. 후에 다툼.

### 26.2 Multi Step Method (Predictor-Corrector Method)

One-step Method ;  $y_{i+1}$  at  $x_{i+1} \leftarrow (x_i, y_i, h_i)$  현재상태 값들 이용 앞으로의 값 예측

Multi-step Method;

$$y_{i+1}$$
 at  $x_{i+1} \leftarrow curve$  by  $(x_i, y_i, x_{i-1}, y_{i-1}, \cdots, h_i, h_{i-1}, \cdots)$  과거의 값과 현재의 값 동시 이용 정확성 향상.
[ Fig. 26.3 ]

### 26.2.1 The Non-self-starting Heun method

$$y_{i+1}^0 = y_i + f(x_i, y_i)h + O(h^2)$$
 ;Predictor – Euler method  $y_{i+1} = y_i + \frac{f(x_i, y_i) + f(x_{i+1}, y_{i+1}^0)}{2}h + O(h^3)$  ;Corrector – Trapezoidal rule efficiency — depend on  $y_{i+1}^0$ : Large error — many iteration  $\therefore$  [Fig. 26.4]

$$y_{i+1}^0 = y_{i-1} + f(x_i, y_i) 2h : \text{ similar to central difference } (+O(h^3))$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \frac{f(x_i, y_i) + f(x_{i+1}, y_{i+1}^0)}{2} h + O(h^3)$$

$$: \text{ average slope(i and i+1 step)}$$

Predictor: 
$$y_{i+1}^0 = y_{i-1}^m + f(x_i, y_i^m) 2h$$

Corrector : 
$$y_{i+1}^j = y_i^m + \frac{f(x_i, y_i^m) + f(x_{i+1}, y_{i+1}^{j-1})}{2}h$$
; Iteration for  $j = 1 - m$ 

$$|\varepsilon_a| = \left|\frac{y_{i+1}^j - y_{i+1}^{j-1}}{y_{i+1}^j}\right| < \varepsilon_s \text{ ; prespecified error tolerance.}$$

$$i = 1 인 경우 y_{i-1} 을 알수 없음. : Non-self starting method$$

### · Derivation and Error Analysis of P - C formulas

For developing higher order multi step methods and estimating their errors.

$$\frac{dy}{dx} = f(x, y)$$

$$\int_{y_{i}}^{y_{i+1}} dy = \int_{x_{i}}^{x_{i+1}} f(x, y) dx$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \int_{x_i}^{x_{i+1}} f(x, y) dx$$
: Numerically integrate

: represents a solution to the ODE if the integral can be evaluated.

### Trapezoidal rule

$$\int_{x_i}^{x_{i+1}} f(x, y) dx \simeq \frac{f(x_i, y_i) + f(x_{i+1}, y_{i+1})}{2} h$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \frac{f(x_i, y_i) + f(x_{i+1}, y_{i+1})}{2}h$$

: The corrector equation of the Heun method

the truncation error of trapezoidal rule(from table 21.2);

$$E_c = -\frac{1}{12} h^3 y^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi_c) = -\frac{1}{12} h^3 f^{\prime\prime}(\xi_c) : \text{error of the corrector}$$

### Midpoint Method

To drive the predictor

$$\int_{y_{i-1}}^{y_{i+1}} dy = \int_{x_{i-1}}^{x_{i+1}} f(x, y) dx$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_{i-1} + \int_{x_{i-1}}^{x_{i+1}} f(x, y) dx$$

$$\int_{x_{i-1}}^{x_{i+1}} f(x, y) dx \approx 2 h f(x_i, y_i) : 1^{st} \text{ order Newton-Cotes open integration}$$

; called midpoint method

$$y_{i+1} = y_{i-1} + f(x_i, y_i)2h$$

$$E_{b} = -\frac{1}{3} h^{3} y^{""}(\xi_{b}) = -\frac{1}{3} h^{3} f^{"}(\xi_{b})$$
: predictor error

### Error estimates

Predictor: True value =  $y_{i+1}^0 + \frac{1}{3} h^3 y^{"}(\xi_p)$ 

Corrector: True value =  $y_{i+1}^m - \frac{1}{12} h^3 y'''(\xi_c)$ 

(Corrector) - (Predictor)

$$\therefore 0 = y_{i+1}^m - y_{i+1}^0 - \frac{5}{12} h^3 y^{(i)}(\xi)$$

$$\frac{y_{i+1}^0 - y_{i+1}^m}{5} = -\frac{1}{12} h^3 y'''(\xi) = E_c \text{ (from [eqn. 26.18])}$$

$$E_c \simeq -\frac{y_{i+1}^m - y_{i+1}^0}{5}$$
; Error of the corrector

Corrector Modifiers ; 
$$y_{i+1}^m \leftarrow y_{i+1}^m - \frac{y_{i+1}^m - y_{i+1}^0}{5}$$

Predictor Modifiers; Number of iteration in corrector ←initial value of predictor.

$$\frac{y_{i+1}^0 - y_{i+1}^m}{5} = -\frac{1}{12} h^3 y'''(\xi) \to \text{ for i-th step,}$$

$$h^3 y'''(\xi) = -\frac{12}{5} (y_i^0 - y_i^m)$$

assume:  $y'''(\xi) \simeq y'''(\xi_p)$  and substituted into [Eq. 26.21]

$$\therefore E_{p} = \frac{1}{3} h^{3} y'''(\xi_{p}) = \frac{4}{5} (y_{i}^{m} - y_{i}^{0})$$

$$y_{i+1}^0 \leftarrow y_{i+1}^0 + \frac{4}{5} (y_i^m - y_i^0)$$

[Fig. 26.5]

Sequence of formulas used to implement nonself-starting Heun method

### 26.2.2 Step size control and computer program

- -- Constant step size; Modification of solution
- -- Variable step size

[Fig. 26.6]

1)  $E_c = \left| \frac{y_{i+1}^m - y_{i+1}^0}{5} \right| > \varepsilon_s$ 2) Corrector step; No. of iteration > 2 decrease the step size

> large h; slowly converge but fewer step for result small h; raped convergence but more step for result

> > → optimum No. of iter.=2

Step size control - step size doubling or halving

### 26.2.3 Integration formulas

- · Non-self-starting Heun method is characteristic of most multi-step methods.; 2-nd order integration formula
- Employ an open integration formula(midpoint method) to make an initial estimate. Predictor step require previous data point
- Corrector step Closed integration formula (Trapezoidal rule)
   applied iteratively to improve the solution
- : Multi-step method -higher order integration formula as P. and C.

### Newton-Cotes formulas

### Open formulas

Predictor; for n equally spaced data points

$$y_{i+1} = y_{i-n} + \int_{x_{i-n}}^{x_{i+1}} f_n(x) dx$$

where  $f_n(x)$  is an n-th order interpolating polynomial.

if n=1, 
$$y_{i+1} = y_{i-1} + 2hf_i$$
  
\nif n=2,  $y_{i+1} = y_{i-2} + \frac{3h}{2}(f_i + f_{i-1})$   
\nif n=3,  $y_{i+1} = y_{i-3} + \frac{4h}{2}(2f_i - f_{i-1} + 2f_{i-2})$  ; [Fig. 26.8 a)]

### Closed formulas;

Corrector; for n equally spaced data points

$$y_{i+1} = y_{i-n+1} + \int_{x_{i-n+1}}^{x_{i+1}} f_n(x) dx$$

if n=1, 
$$y_{i+1} = y_i + \frac{h}{2}(f_i + f_{i+1})$$

if n=2, 
$$y_{i+1} = y_{i-1} + \frac{h}{3}(f_{i+1} + 4f_i + f_{i-1})$$
; Simpson's 1/3 rule [Fig.26.8 b)]

### Adams formulas

### Open formula(Adams-Bashforth)

Taylor series

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + f_i h + \frac{f_i}{2} h^2 + \frac{f_i^{"}}{3!} h^3 + \cdots$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + h \left( f_i + \frac{f_i}{2} h + \frac{f_i^{"}}{6} h^2 + \cdots \right)$$

$$f_i^{"} = \frac{f_i - f_{i-1}}{h} + \frac{f_i^{"}}{2} h + O(h^2) \quad \text{; From Taylor series}$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + h \left\{ f_i + \frac{h}{2} \left[ \frac{f_i - f_{i-1}}{h} + \frac{f_i^{"}}{2} h + O(h^2) \right] + \frac{h^2}{6} f_i^{"} + \cdots \right\}$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + h \left( \frac{3}{2} f_i - \frac{1}{2} f_{i-1} \right) + \frac{5}{12} h^3 f_i^{"} + O(h^4)$$

; 2-nd order open Adams formular

For higher-order

$$y_{i+1} = y_i + h \sum_{k=0}^{n-1} \beta_k f_{i-k} + O(h^{n+1})$$

where  $\beta_k$ 's are the value in the [Table 26.1] [Fig.26.9 a)]

### Closed formula(Adams-Moulton)

Taylor series

$$y_{i} = y_{i+1} - f_{i+1}h + \frac{f_{i+1}}{2}h^{2} - \frac{f_{i+1}}{3!}h^{3} + \cdots$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_{i} + h(f_{i+1} - \frac{f_{i+1}}{2}h + \frac{f_{i+1}}{6}h^{2} - \cdots)$$

$$f_{i+1} = \frac{f_{i+1} - f_{i}}{h} + \frac{f_{i+1}}{2}h + O(h^{2}) \quad \text{; From Taylor series}$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_{i} + h\{f_{i+1} - \frac{h}{2}\left[\frac{f_{i+1} - f_{i}}{h} + \frac{f_{i+1}}{2}h + O(h^{2})\right] + \frac{h^{2}}{6}f_{i+1}^{*} - \cdots\}$$

$$y_{i+1} = y_{i} + h(\frac{1}{2}f_{i+1} - \frac{1}{2}f_{i}) + \frac{1}{12}h^{3}f_{i}^{*} - O(h^{4}); \text{ 2-nd order closed form.}$$
For higher-order;  $f_{i} = \frac{f_{i+1} - f_{i}}{h}$ 

$$y_{i+1} = y_{i} + h\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\beta_{k}f_{i+1-k} + O(h^{n+1}); \quad \beta_{k}\text{'s are in the [Table 26.2]}$$
[Fig.26.9 b)]

### 26.2.4 Higher-order multistep method

### Milne's method

Predictor; three point Newton-Cotes open formula

$$y_{i+1}^0 = y_{i-3}^m + \frac{4h}{3}(2f_i^m - f_{i-1}^m + 2f_{i-2}^m)$$

Corrector; Simpson's 1/3 rule

$$y_{i+1}^{j} = y_{i-1}^{m} + \frac{h}{3} (f_{i-1}^{m} + 4f_{i}^{m} + f_{i+1}^{j-1})$$

Modifier ;

$$E_{\mathfrak{p}} \simeq \frac{28}{29} (y_i^m - y_i^0)$$

$$E_c \simeq -\frac{1}{29} (y_{i+1}^m - y_{i+1}^0)$$

### Fourth-order Adams method

Predictor; 4-th order Adams-Bashforth

$$y_{i+1}^0 = y_i^m + h(\frac{55}{24}f_i^m - \frac{59}{24}f_{i-1}^m + \frac{37}{24}f_{i-2}^m - \frac{9}{24}f_{i-3}^m)$$

Corrector; 4-th order Adams-Moulton

$$y_{i+1}^{j} = y_{i-1}^{m} + h(\frac{9}{24}f_{i+1}^{j-1} + \frac{19}{24}f_{i}^{m} - \frac{5}{24}f_{i-1}^{m} + \frac{1}{24}f_{i-2}^{m})$$

Modifier

$$E_{p} \simeq \frac{251}{270} (y_{i}^{m} - y_{i}^{0})$$

$$E_c \simeq -\frac{19}{270} (y_{i+1}^m - y_{i+1}^0)$$

Stability of Multistep method

[ Fig. 26.10 ] → instability of Milne's method.

### Hamming method

modify Milne's method for corrector: stable corrector.

$$y_{i+1}^{j} = \frac{9y_{i}^{m} - y_{i-2}^{m} + 3h(f_{i+1}^{j-1} + 2f_{i}^{m} - f_{i-1}^{m})}{8}$$

Truncation error;  $E_c = \frac{1}{40} h^5 f^{(4)}(\xi_c)$ 

Modifiers;

$$E_{p} \simeq \frac{9}{121} (y_i^m - y_i^0)$$

$$E_c \simeq -\frac{112}{121}(y_{i+1}^m - y_{i+1}^0)$$

### Chapter 27. Boundary-Value and Eigenvalue Problem

For n-th order eqn., n conditions are required.

- · Initial value problem; Chapter 25 and 26.
- · Bonudary value problem; specified at the extreme points or boundaris. Solution Method; Shooting method, Finite difference method(FDM)

  [Fig.27.1]
- · Determination of the Eigenvalue;

special type of boundary value problem

### 27.1 General Methods for Boundary-Value Problems

model eqn.; 
$$\frac{d^2T}{dx^2} + h'(T_a - T) = 0$$

Boundary conditions

$$T(0) = T_1, \quad T(L) = T_2$$
[Fig. 27.2]

Exact solution; 
$$T=73.4523~e^{0.1x}-53.4523~e^{-0.1x}+20$$
, for  $T_a=20$ ,  $T_1=40$ ,  $T_2=200$  and  $h=0.01$ 

### 27.1.1 The shooting method

- · Converting the boundary value problem into an equivalent initial value problem.
  - · Trial-and-error approach
  - · Solving method; [Example. 27.1]

$$\frac{dT}{dx} = z$$

$$\frac{dz}{dx} = h' (T - T_a)$$

First guess z(0)=A, and compute  $T(L)_1$ 

- integrating above eqn.s simultaneously using a 4-th order RK method.

Second guess z(0)=B, and compute  $T(L)_2$ , then interpolate z(0)

[Fig. 27.3]

Nonlinear problem : 반복계산(Method for Solutions of Equation)

Higher order ODE : 사용 복잡함. [Fig. 27.4]

### 27.1.2 Finite difference methods

· Differential form -> difference form : Numerical difference

$$\frac{d^2T}{dx^2} = \frac{T_{i+1} - 2T_i + T_{i-1}}{\Delta x^2}$$
; centered difference

$$\frac{T_{i+1} - 2T_i + T_{i+1}}{\Delta x^2} - h(T_i - T_a) = 0$$

$$T_{i-1} + (2 + h' \triangle x^2) T_i - T_{i+1} = h' \triangle x^2 T_a$$

and

$$\begin{bmatrix} 2+\alpha & -1 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 \\ -1 & 2+\alpha & -1 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & -1 & 2+\alpha & -1 & 0 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & -1 & 2+\alpha & -1 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 0 & -1 & 2+\alpha & -1 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & -1 & 2+\alpha & -1 \\ 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & -1 & 2+\alpha \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} T_1 \\ T_2 \\ T_3 \\ T_4 \\ T_5 \\ T_6 \\ T_7 \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} \alpha T_a + T_0 \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \\ \alpha T_a \end{bmatrix}$$

where  $\alpha = h^{\prime} \triangle x^2$ 

[ Table 27.1 ] → Error of FDM > Error of Shooting Method

FDM: easy to more complex cases. nonlinear or higher order.

### 27.2 Eigenvalue Problems

or Charateristic value problems

; special class of boundary value problems. (vibration, elasticity and other oscillating problems)

### 27.2.1 Mathematical background

· Set of linear algebraic equation of the general form

 $[A]{X} = {C}$ ; Nonhomogeneous

If lineary independent, exist unique solution

 $[A]{X} = 0$ ; Homogeneous

Not unique solution; The x's can be satisfied by various combinations of values

Relationship among the x's with various combination of values.

$$(a_{11} - \lambda)x_1 + a_{12}x_2 + \dots + a_{1n}x_n = 0$$

$$a_{21}x_1 + (a_{22} - \lambda)x_2 + \dots + a_{2n}x_n = 0$$

:

$$a_{n1}x_1 + a_{n2}x_2 + \dots + (a_{nn} - \lambda) = 0$$

where  $\lambda$  : eigenvalue or characteristic value ; unknown parameters A solution  $\{X\}$  : eigenvector

$$\lceil \lceil A \rceil - \lambda \lceil I \rceil \rceil \langle X \rangle = 0$$

The determinant

$$|[A] - \lambda[I]| = 0$$
 for non-trivial solution (solutions other than all  $x's=0$ )

Expanding the determinant yields a polynomial in  $\lambda$ . The roots of this polynomial are the solutions for the eigenvalues  $\lambda$ .

### 27.2.2 Physical background

Spring vibration [Fig. 27.5]

Governing ean.

$$m_1 \frac{d^2 x_1}{dt^2} - k(-2x_1 + x_2) = 0$$

$$m_2 \frac{d^2 x_2}{dt^2} - k(x_1 - 2x_2) = 0$$

sol

$$x_i = A_i \sin(\omega t)$$
, where  $\omega = \frac{2\pi}{T_p}$ ,  $T_p$  is period.

$$(\frac{2k}{m_1} - \omega^2)A_1 - \frac{k}{m_1}A_2 = 0$$

$$\frac{-k}{m_2}A_1 + (\frac{2k}{m_2} - \omega^2)A_2 = 0$$

The eigenvalues are  $\omega$ s and the eigenvectors are  $[A_1, A_2]^T$  [Fig. 27.6]

### 27.2.3 A Boundary value problem

Buckling [Fig. 27.7]

Governing eqn.

$$\frac{d^2y}{dx^2} - p^2y = 0 \text{ , where } p^2 = \frac{P}{EI}$$

B. C. 
$$y(0) = 0$$
,  $y(L) = 0$ 

Sol. 
$$A \sin(pL) = 0$$
,  $A \neq 0$ ,  $\therefore pL = n \pi$  for  $n = 1, 2, 3, \cdots$ 

$$\therefore p = \frac{n\pi}{L} \text{ ; eigenvalue}$$

$$\therefore P = \frac{n^2 \pi^2 EI}{L} \quad \text{for } n = 1, 2, 3, \dots$$

[ Fig. 27.8 ]

### 27.2.4 The polynomial method

$$\frac{d^2y}{dx^2} - p^2y = 0 \text{ , where } p^2 = \frac{P}{EI}$$

by FDM

$$\frac{y_{i+1} - 2y_i + y_{i+1}}{h^2} + p^2 y_i = 0$$

$$y_{i-1} - (2 - h^2 p^2) y_i + y_{i+1} = 0$$

$$\begin{bmatrix} 2-h^2p^2 & -1 & 0 & 0 \\ -1 & 2-h^2p^2 & -1 & 0 \\ 0 & -1 & 2-h^2p^2 & -1 \\ 0 & 0 & -1 & 2-h^2p^2 \end{bmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} y_1 \\ y_2 \\ y_3 \\ y_4 \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} 0 \\ 0 \\ 0 \\ 0 \end{pmatrix}$$

; eigenvalue problem

### The Fadeev-Leverrier method;

generate the coefficients  $p_i$  of polynomial

 $(-1)^n(\lambda^n - p_{n-1}\lambda^{n-1} - \dots - p_1\lambda - p_0) = 0$  where n is the order of matrix. which result from the expansion of the determinant of the

$$[[A] - \lambda [I]]\{X\} = 0$$

Procedure;

$$[B]_{n-1} = [A]$$

 $p_{n-1} = tr[B]_{n-1}$ ; trace of  $[B]_{n-1}$ : sum of the diagonal coefficients.

$$[B]_{n-2} = [A][[B]_{n-1} - p_{n-1}[I]]$$

$$p_{n-2} = \frac{1}{2} tr[B]_{n-2}$$

$$[B]_{n-3} = [A][[B]_{n-2} - p_{n-2}[I]]$$

$$p_{n-3} = \frac{1}{3} tr[B]_{n-3} \qquad \cdots$$

$$[B]_0 = [A][[B]_1 - p_1[I]]$$

$$p_0 = \frac{1}{n} \operatorname{tr}[B]_0$$

$$[A]^{-1} = \frac{1}{p_0}[[B]_1 - p_1[I]];$$
 inverse of matrix [A]: [Example 21.6]

- · Determining of the Eigenvalues

  as the roots of the characteristic polynomial
- · Bairstow's method

### 27.2.5 The power method

- · Iterative approach
- · can be employed to determine the largest eigenvalue, with small modification, smallest and intermediate eigenvalue.
- · additional benefit : eigenvector is obtained as a by-product of method
- · Determination of the largest eigenvalue
- The system being analyzed must be expressed in the form.

$$[A]{X} = \lambda{X}$$

Start with 
$$\{X\}_0 = [1, 1, 1]^T$$
  
 $\{Y\}_0 = [A]\{X\}_0$ 

Then, the  $\{Y\}_0$  is normalized by largest value  $\lambda_1$  among its components.

$$\{Y\}_0 = \lambda_1 \{X\}_1$$

And do second iteration with

 $\{Y\}_1 = [A]\{X\}_1$  and then, normalized by largest value  $\lambda_2$ 

$$\{Y\}_1 = \lambda_2 \{X\}_2$$

Thus, the normalizing factor  $\lambda_i$  is converging on the value of the largest eigenvalue.

Convergence criterion : 
$$|\varepsilon_a| = \left| \frac{\lambda_i - \lambda_{i-1}}{\lambda_i} \right| \langle \varepsilon_s$$

· Determination of the smallest eigenvalue

The system is expressed in the form.

$$[A]^{-1}\{X\} = \lambda\{X\}$$

Start with 
$$\{X\}_0 = [1, 1, 1, \cdots]^T$$

$$\{Y\}_0 = [A]^{-1}\{X\}_0$$
 normalize as  $\{Y\}_0 = \lambda_1\{X\}_1$ 

$$\{Y\}_1 = [A]^{-1}\{X\}_1$$
 normalize as  $\{Y\}_1 = \lambda_2\{X\}_2$ , ...

Thus, the normalizing factor  $\lambda_i$  is converging on the value of the smallest eigenvalue.

### · Determination of the intermediate eigenvalue

After finding the largest eigenvalue, determine the next highest eigenvalue Deflation; removing the largest known eigenvalue.

Hotelling's method; designed for symmetric matrices.

$$\{X\}_{i}^{T}\{X\}_{j} = \begin{cases} 0 & \text{for } i \neq j \\ 1 & \text{for } i = j \end{cases}$$
; the orthogonality of the eigenvector.

$$[A]_2 = [A]_1 - \lambda_1 \{X\}_1 \{X\}_1^T$$

 $[A]_1$ : orginal matrix

 $\lambda_1$ : largest eigen value and  $\{X\}_1$ : its eigenvector

And then, start with  $\{X\}_{2}^{0} = [1, 1, 1, \cdots]^{T}$ 

$$[A]_{2}\{X\}_{2}^{j} = \lambda_{2}^{j+1}\{X\}_{2}^{j+1}$$

To show this,

$$[A]_{2}\{X\}_{1} = [A]_{1}\{X\}_{1} - \lambda_{1}\{X\}_{1}\{X\}_{1}^{T}\{X\}_{1}$$

$$[A]_{2}{X}_{1} = [A]_{1}{X}_{1} - \lambda_{1}{X}_{1} = 0$$
